# Phase 9: store-agnostic promotion in longterm-mem

Scope (L42, L44): this feature closes in Phase 9 with T1-T6 and T10 (T6 replaces C22-FU T4); T7-T9 and T11-T20 and the Specs only they carry move to the first feature of the next phase, which takes this document as its specification.

Objective of this feature (T1-T6 and T10, delivered in Phase 9, L44): longterm-mem promotes memories into the vault without knowing which store they come from: one memory model with a `source` + `source_id` identity, one page-identity reader, and one promotion walk over a `Source` port, each store an adapter. Then (T4, T5, T10, T6) memorycard becomes the only promotion source, `doctor` and `reconcile` read memorycard identity, and every legacy page takes its memorycard identity in a one-time migration the owner runs.

Objective of the next phase's first feature (T7-T9 and T11-T20, specified here, not delivered by this feature): since L25 and L27, memorycard is the only memory store: Engram is removed from all of longterm-mem (promotion and retrieval) and from the engine's memory plan, projection gate and skills candidates, every page takes its memorycard identity, and `internal/engram` is deleted last; forecast (L28): ~4500-6500 authored changed lines of production and docs over T4-T20 as measured when it was written (L28), deletions counted, tests on top; unmeasured, a planning figure. Since L44 it covers T4-T20 minus T4, T5, T6 and T10, which are delivered in Phase 9: the next phase takes T7-T9 and T11-T20, and the figure is not re-measured for them.

Branch `feat/phase9-store-agnostic-promotion` (from origin/main `9ad3b156`; one branch per task, T3 on `feat/phase9-sap-t3-promotion-walk`); delivery strategy `ask-on-risk`.
Test runner: `go test` in `longterm-mem/` and in `engine/` (`./synctrigger/...`); the owner alone runs anything against the live vault or the live memorycard store.

## Specs

Line numbers of S1-S9 are as read on this branch at `9ad3b156` (L3, L12); those of S4-S8, S10 and S11 as rewritten in L26 are as read on the T3 branch tip (L26). The owner's decisions D1-D6 (L4-L11), the coordinator's no-change default for D7 (L11) and the answer to the L12 question (L17) are folded in. L25 (owner, verbatim: "CAMBIALO TODO POR memorycard che ya no sigas metiendo cosas de engram lo sacamos") retires Engram as a promotion source: S1-S3 stay as the record of T1-T3 (met, and still the base of what follows), and S10 says which parts of S2, S3 and S5 it replaces; S4-S8 are rewritten, S10 and S11 are new (L26). L27 (owner, verbatim: "de todos lados") decides R1: S10's scope is all of longterm-mem, and S12-S16 (L28) carry retrieval, the deletion of `internal/engram` and the three engine consumers. The owner decided R2-R13 in L29-L40; they are folded into the specs and tasks below (L41), and no product decision of this feature is open. Ending the `engram_id`/`memory_id` transition of R8 (L35) is a later owner decision, outside this feature.

- **S1 — One memory model.** Owner (L1), verbatim: "y pero todo eso esta acoplado a memorycard??", and "si" to "La versión desacoplada sería un solo modelo `Memory`, cuya identidad sea `source` + `source_id`."
  - Found (read): promotion speaks two models, `memory.Observation` (`longterm-mem/internal/memory/observation.go:22-35`) and `memory.CardMemory` (`internal/memory/card.go:12-42`), whose comment says promotion "keeps the two apart because a page of the vault names its source (engram_id or memorycard_id)" (`card.go:9-11`).
  - Scope: `internal/memory` gains `Memory`, whose identity is a source plus a source id (one identity value, assumption: named `Identity`), carrying what promotion reads of any store: project, type, title, body, topic key, pinned, status (`active`, `superseded` or `archived`), the successor's identity and title, revision, and its lineage: the identities the same piece of memory has in another store (today memorycard's `import_map` row of source `engram`, `CardMemory.EngramID`, and the same-content Engram twins, `CardMemory.SameContentEngramIDs`). R-007's `Eligible` (`internal/promote/eligible.go`) takes a `Memory`. `CardMemory` is removed once nothing reads it.
  - Not changed (assumption: L1 is about promotion): `memory.Observation` stays the model of the readers that are not promotion (`query`, `staleness`, `skillstale`, the MCP `get` tool, `doctor`'s live-observation listing); the query and embedding-index `engram_id` fields (`internal/query/result.go:56`, `internal/vecindex/index.go:60`) are search results, not page identity.
  - Acceptance: after T3, `rg -n 'CardMemory|memory\.Observation' longterm-mem/internal/promote --glob '!*_test.go'` prints nothing; `go vet ./...` and `go test -count=1 ./...` in `longterm-mem/` pass (`GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.1`, `TMPDIR` outside any repository, C22-FU L12).
- **S2 — Each store is an adapter.** Owner (L1), verbatim, from the approved proposal: "y cada store sería solo un adaptador."
  - (a) A port owned by `internal/promote` (assumption: named `Source`) serves one store's memories as `Memory`: a project's candidates, every memory of a project whatever its status (what propagation reads), and one memory by id.
  - (b) Engram adapter: source `engram`, revision from `revision_count`, status `archived` for a soft-deleted observation and `superseded`, with its successor, for the older side of a `supersedes` edge, newer by `created_at`: the rule of `resolveStatus` (`internal/promote/propagate.go:185-230`) moves behind the adapter with its behavior unchanged. It stays read-only (Engram is frozen read-only since 2026-11-04, L2).
  - (c) memorycard adapter: source `memorycard`, status, `superseded_by` and revision as `internal/memorycard/store.go` maps them today (one mapping, `memorySelect`/`scanMemory`, C22-FU L12).
  - (d) Lineage, both ways: a memorycard memory's lineage names the Engram observation its `import_map` row names, and the same-content twins `withSameContent` finds (`cmd/longterm-mem/cmd_sync.go`, C22-FU L14); an Engram observation's lineage names the memorycard memory imported from it, through the reverse `import_map` lookup `MemoryByEngramID` built on `feat/phase9-c22fu-t4-migration` (`43a75364`, C22-FU L20), reused, not rewritten. Lineage is composed in `cmd/longterm-mem`, so `internal/engram` never imports memorycard and `internal/promote` imports no store (today only `cmd/longterm-mem` imports `internal/memorycard`, L2).
  - Acceptance: adapter tests over scratch SQLite databases built from each store's schema compare each mapped `Memory` whole (`reflect.DeepEqual`), including the supersession and soft-delete cases `internal/promote/propagate_test.go` pins today and both lineage directions; `go list -deps ./internal/promote` names neither `internal/engram` nor `internal/memorycard`; the module's architecture guards pass.
- **S3 — One promotion walk.** Owner (L1), verbatim, from the approved proposal: "habría un solo recorrido".
  - Found: two walks and two decisions, `Sync`/`Plan`/`decidePromotion` (`internal/promote/sync.go:83-260`) and `SyncCards`/`PlanCards`/`walkCards`/`decideCard`/`PromoteCard` (`sync_cards.go:51-167`, `card.go:77-130`); two explicit promotes, `ExplicitPromote` and `ExplicitPromoteCard` (`card.go:57-66`).
  - Scope: one walk over a source's candidates serves `sync` and `sync --dry-run`, and its one decision serves explicit `promote` (an explicit promote overrides eligibility, as today). For each memory: ineligible by R-007, skipped; its own page (found by its identity) at its revision or newer, nothing written; a newer revision, updated in place, with R-030 local-edit precedence; no own page but a page carrying one of its lineage identities, bridged: skipped and counted, never a failure, nothing written, exit 0 (C22-FU FU3, verbatim: "skip and count a bridged memory in the sync summary (exit 0)", now for every source); a page that could not be read and no readable page deciding it, withheld; otherwise a new page. The project-move rule ("An Observation Moved Between Projects Leaves a Superseded Page", `openspec/specs/longterm-mem-promotion/spec.md:230-348`) is keyed by identity (assumption: it applies to any source).
  - Output: every existing command form keeps its stdout, stderr and exit code. Assumption: an Engram observation newly found bridged is counted in the dry run's skip count and named nowhere else, so plain `sync`'s summary keeps its words.
  - Acceptance: RED first: in a scratch world, an Engram observation whose memorycard lineage memory owns a page is not promoted by Engram `sync` (the defect L2 records: "would promote 0 observation(s)" before, "would promote 10 observation(s)" after); every existing test of `sync`, `promote`, card promote and `sync --source memorycard` passes with its assertions unchanged; differential against a `git archive` copy of `9ad3b156` in `mktemp -d`: `sync`, `sync --dry-run`, `sync --source engram`, `sync --source memorycard`, `sync --source memorycard --dry-run`, `promote --id N`, `promote --source memorycard --id N` and the MCP `promote` tool, comparing stdout, stderr, exit code and the vault's files except wall-clock stamps: all SAME; the requirements "Sync Promotes Unpromoted-or-Revised Observations" and "Explicit Promote Surface" of the promotion spec state the store-agnostic walk, each with a "(Previously: ...)" note (the pattern of `spec.md:819-820`). This feature owns that wording, C22-FU S1 (f) included (D6, L11, verbatim: "si continua"); C22-FU T5 keeps only memorycard-specific wording.
- **S4 — One propagation walk.** The same L1 quote as S3; rewritten by L26 after L25.
  - Found (T3 tip): propagation is still two walks over `Source.Memories` (L20): the Engram dialect `Propagate`/`decidePatch` (`internal/promote/propagate.go`, `StatusUnknown` handled at `:198`), which counts every visited page and never brings a page back from `archived`, and memorycard's `PropagateCards` (`propagate_cards.go`, `StatusUnknown` at `:108`), which visits only a page whose frontmatter would change and brings a restored memory's page back (C22-FU L19-fix, 1 and 3).
  - Scope: one walk, memorycard's, patches a page's `status` and `related` from its memory's status and successor, frontmatter only, even on a locally edited page, the patch recorded in the precedence store (R-033), through the one definition of a patch, `patchedFrontmatter`. D3 (L6, owner verbatim: "adaptarla") is met by memorycard's rule being the only one: only a page whose frontmatter actually changes is counted as patched, and a page whose memory is active again is brought back (with C22-FU L19-fix 3's guard). The Engram dialect is deleted with the Engram source (S10). Plain `sync`'s `patched N page(s)` becomes memorycard's count, with S10's switch.
  - Not changed: a page that still carries only the legacy `engram_*` keys (before T6) is not a memorycard memory's own page, so the walk does not patch it, as memorycard's walk does not today (`ownPage`, `internal/promote/page_scan.go:155-162`). Engram is frozen, so no status of an observation changes any more (L2).
  - Acceptance: `rg -n 'func (\(w \*Writer\) )?Propagate\b|eachPatchTarget|decidePatch' longterm-mem/internal/promote --glob '!*_test.go'` prints only the one walk; every existing memorycard propagation test passes unchanged; the Engram propagation tests are deleted with their dialect, each named in the Log; "Sync Rebuilds the Index and Records Sync State" and "Soft-Delete and Supersession Propagate to the Vault" state the one walk over memorycard, each with a "(Previously: ...)" note (this feature owns that wording, D6).
- **S5 — Stores are named only at the composition root.** The same L1 quote as S2; rewritten by L26 after L25.
  - Found (T3 tip): the closed `--source engram|memorycard` of `promote` (`cmd/longterm-mem/cmd_promote.go:36`, `:41-42`, constants `:91`) and `sync` (`cmd_sync.go:30`, `:35-36`), default `engram`; the MCP `promote` tool's `source` (`internal/mcpserver/server.go:164`, `:171-172`: "the store to promote from: engram (the default) or memorycard") and its `engram_id` field (`:171`: "the Engram observation id to promote; with source memorycard, the memorycard memory id").
  - Scope: one registry of sources in `cmd/longterm-mem`, holding one entry, `memorycard` (L25); `promote --source`, `sync --source` and the MCP tool's `source` choose a registered adapter by name; an unknown name, `engram` included, is exit 2 with the message the existing tests pin, its list rendered from the registry, so it reads `longterm-mem: sync: --source "notes" is not a store: use memorycard` where C22-FU L14 pinned `... use engram or memorycard`. A new store is one adapter plus one registry entry, with no change in `internal/promote` (L1, "cada store sería solo un adaptador"; L25 does not revoke it).
  - Decided (R2, L29, owner verbatim: "recomeneado"): `promote --id N` with no `--source`, and the MCP `promote` tool with no `source`, are refused with exit 2 (the MCP tool returns the same refusal as a tool error) and ask for `--source memorycard`, so no id silently changes meaning (today they name an Engram observation, `cmd_promote.go:34`: "Engram observation id (required)"). Assumption, the words: `longterm-mem: promote: --source is required: use --source memorycard`. `sync` keeps a default (memorycard, S10 (b)): it takes no id.
  - The MCP `promote` tool's id (R8, L35, owner verbatim: "3"): the input accepts `engram_id` (the consumed field, `server.go:171`) and a new store-agnostic `memory_id`, both naming a memorycard memory id, as S12 (h) shapes them.
  - Acceptance: the existing test that `internal/promote` imports no store adapter passes (`TestPromotionImportsNoStoreAdapter`, T1); the unknown-source tests pass with the one changed list, each named in the Log; help text, `longterm-mem/README.md`, `docs/sync-from-memorycard.md` and `docs/promote-from-memorycard.md` name memorycard as the one registered store.
- **S6 — Store-agnostic page identity.** Owner (L1), verbatim: "Las páginas llevarían esa identidad genérica" (from the approved proposal) and "si no se usa para que lo vamos a conservar" (on keeping `engram_id` on migrated pages). A persisted format change of existing pages (L2: "the vault holds ~600 pages carrying `engram_id`, 51 + 10 with `**Source-Id**`, and the live count must be measured, not assumed").
  - (a) Reader. One reader of a page's identity and revision, used by every consumer of page identity: the promoted-page scans of `internal/promote/address.go` (including pages of another project), the card scan (`card.go:188-260`), update corroboration (`update.go:343-424`), `reconcile` (`frontmatterRevision`, `reconcile.go:193`, `:217`), the project move (`writer.go:347-367`), `PageObservationID` (`eligible.go:55`), `doctor` (`internal/ops/doctor_eligibility.go`, the precedence-sidecar check, `internal/ops/doctor.go`) and propagation. It reads the store-agnostic keys `source`, `source_id`, `source_revision` and `source_type` (D1, L4, owner verbatim: "la recomendada") and the legacy keys `engram_id`/`engram_revision`/`engram_type` and `memorycard_id`/`memorycard_revision`/`memorycard_type` as the identity they name. Reading only: no page changes.
  - (b) Writer. Every page the writer creates or fully re-renders from then on (a new page, an update in place) carries only the store-agnostic keys, with `source: memorycard` (after S10 no other store writes a page); a status patch changes `status` and `related` only and leaves a page's identity lines alone, since only the one-time migration of (c) rewrites the identity of an existing page. (Previously: "a new page, an update in place, a status patch"; the status patch was taken out by the coordinator's decision, L64.) `engram_sync_id` is not written (found: no reader reads it back from a page; update corroboration only blanks it, `update.go:424`).
  - (c) Migration, once (rewritten by L26 after L25: every page takes its memorycard identity, where C22-FU FU1 named only the ten). A command (assumption: its form), dry run by default and `--apply` explicit, rewrites every page carrying legacy keys to the store-agnostic keys, keeping its address, `created`, body and every other key, and rewrites only that page's precedence-entry frontmatter hash in `.raw/.longterm-mem-manifest.json`. A page carrying `memorycard_*` keys keeps its memorycard identity. A page carrying `engram_id` N takes the identity of the memorycard memory imported from observation N, read from memorycard's `import_map` alone (`Store.MemoryByEngramID`, `internal/memorycard/store.go:143-148`, the memory of the first part); it reads no Engram database. Its `source_revision` is that memory's revision at migration (assumption: so the migration changes no body and (d) holds; a memory edited in memorycard after its import keeps its Engram-era body until its next revision). The ten pre-memorycard pages (C22-FU S2: `c-000112` to `c-000121`) are among them (C22-FU FU1, verbatim: "mandale"). Before its first write it backs up every file it will rewrite (the vault's `wiki/memory/` and the sidecar are not git-tracked, C22-FU L17). It refuses by name, goes on, and exits 6 for: a page that differs from its precedence entry (a hand edit, R-030), a page with no entry, a page whose `engram_id` no `import_map` row names, and a page whose memorycard memory already has a page of its own (two pages for one memory); those last two are left for the owner and never matched by content hash (R5, L32). The dry run prints the count of each refusal. A second run changes nothing. Reading rules (decided in L66, L68 and L69, coordinator decisions D-a to D-d): (i) a page is *readable* when it starts at byte 0 with a line of `---` and has a closing line of `---` (both ending in a line feed, nothing after the dashes) and the shared page reader splits it at the same place; a frontmatter line that ends in `---` makes it unreadable; (ii) a page whose first non-blank line is `---` (after a BOM, blank lines, spaces, tabs and carriage returns are ignored) but that is not readable has *unreadable frontmatter*: trailing spaces and tabs on its opening and closing `---` lines are ignored, it is scanned up to its first closing `---` line, or to the end when there is none, and it is counted and refused by name when a top-level legacy key is found, never rewritten, and ignored otherwise; (iii) a page whose first non-blank line is not `---` has *no frontmatter* and is ignored, its text never scanned, so a legacy key before the first `---` is ignored (coordinator decision, accepted as part of D-c, no follow-up); (iv) a page with a legacy key and no project belongs to no project's run: every run notes it once on stdout and it makes no exit code of its own, and the rehearsal script's vault-wide count includes it.
  - (d) Never promoted twice: on the rehearsal copies, `sync --dry-run` (memorycard, the one source after S10) predicts the same promotions after the migration as before it.
  - (e) Existing pages keep resolving: addresses do not change, so every wikilink holds; the index rebuild runs on the copy; a refused hand-edited page stays byte-identical; explicit `promote --source memorycard --id M` of a migrated page's memory answers `already promoted as <address>`; `doctor` reports the same findings before and after (it joins by memorycard identity, S11).
  - (f) The owner alone rehearses on copies (a script, as C22-FU T4's `t4-rehearsal.sh`) and alone runs the live `--apply`; the live counts, the backup path and before/after digests go in the Log. This replaces C22-FU T4 (D5, L10, owner verbatim: "lo recomendado"): `feat/phase9-c22fu-t4-migration` is not merged, its code and rehearsal script are reused here, and C22-FU T4 closes as replaced by T6.
  - Acceptance: tests over a scratch vault and scratch stores for (a)-(e); the promotion spec's "Contract-Conformant Page Emission", "Update-in-Place on Revision" and "Local-Edit Precedence" name the store-agnostic keys, each with a "(Previously: ...)" note; `longterm-mem/README.md` and the docs describe the page format and the migration; the rehearsal script checked with `bash -n` and run only by the owner.
- **S7 — Legacy keys retired.** Owner (L1), verbatim: "si no se usa para que lo vamos a conservar"; D2 (L5), verbatim: "retirarlas".
  - Scope: after the owner's live T6 migration, when its dry run on the live vault reports zero pages carrying legacy keys, the reader of S6 (a) stops reading them. Assumption (from the same L1 rule): the one-time migration command is retired with them, since it then has nothing left to migrate. Added by L26 (same rule, after L25): `engram` stops being a page family (`pageSources`, `internal/promote/page_scan.go:17`; `engramSource` and `legacyKeys`, `page_identity.go:26-34`), and the memorycard adapter stops naming the Engram observation of a memory's `import_map` row as its lineage (S10 (c)), since no page carries an Engram identity any more.
  - Acceptance: the owner's live dry-run line ("0" legacy pages) recorded in the Log before this task starts; `rg -n 'engram_id|memorycard_id|engram_revision|memorycard_revision|engram_type|memorycard_type' longterm-mem/internal longterm-mem/cmd --glob '!*_test.go'` prints only the non-page uses S1 leaves untouched (the transitional `engram_id` fields of `query`, `get` and `promote`, S12 (h), L35); a test pins that a page carrying only legacy keys is no longer a promoted page.
- **S8 — The hook-driven sync reads its source from configuration.** Owner (L1), verbatim, from the approved proposal: "El hook leería el source de la configuración, en vez de tenerlo fijo en el código." and "no tocar el hook hasta diseñar el modelo agnóstico al store."
  - Found: `engine/synctrigger` starts `longterm-mem` with `[]string{syncCommand}` (`engine/synctrigger/synctrigger.go:303`), `syncCommand = "sync"` (`:115`), and maps exit 4 to `failure:engram-unavailable` (`:101`, `:335-336`); plain `sync` reads Engram (`cmd_sync.go:31`).
  - Rewritten by L26 after L25. D4 (L7, owner verbatim: "en el vault pero hay que asegurarnos que cada proyecto tenga un vault") placed the hook's source on the project's registry row; with one store there is no source left to choose, and plain `sync` syncs memorycard (S10). Decided (R4, L31, owner verbatim: "1"): the optional registry `source` field of D4 is not built while memorycard is the only store; this supersedes D4. L17's question for T8 ("whether that default stays `engram`") is answered by L25: the default is memorycard. "hay que asegurarnos que cada proyecto tenga un vault" is S9.
  - Scope: `engine/synctrigger` keeps running plain `sync` (`synctrigger.go:303`) and names no store in its code or comments; its classification of the child's exit codes and of the `could not be resolved from the working directory` stderr marker (`:132`) holds for memorycard's sync. Exit 4 keeps its number: it already means "the memory store could not be opened" for memorycard too (`cmd/longterm-mem/app.go:93`, `:112`, `exitEngramUnavailable`, `exit_codes.go:42`); the constant gets a store-agnostic name (internal).
  - Decided (R3, L30, owner verbatim: "1"; supersedes D7, L11, for the label): the log kind of exit 4 becomes `failure:store-unavailable` (today `failure:engram-unavailable`, `synctrigger.go:101`, `:336`, golden `engine/cmd/testdata/synctrigger-golden/the-child-reports-engram-unavailable.golden`). Its "(Previously: ...)" note goes wherever the label is specified or documented.
  - The live switch happens with S10's task, not here: the owner's live read-only `sync --dry-run --source memorycard` first (the C22-FU L15/L17 rule).
  - Acceptance: the existing `engine/synctrigger` tests pass (`go test -count=1 ./synctrigger/...` from `engine/`), except the label, whose test and golden now say `failure:store-unavailable` (named in the Log); a hook-path test runs plain `sync` against a scratch memorycard store and a scratch vault and is classified as a success, exit 3 as `skip:no-vault`, and a missing memorycard database as exit 4; `rg -in engram engine/synctrigger --glob '!*_test.go'` prints nothing.
- **S9 — Every project has a default vault.** Owner, verbatim (L8): "esaue deberia de tener un vault de longterm-mem por defecto cada proyecto vos no tendrias que decidir crearlo o no"; (L9) "uno por proyecto dentro del directorio raiz de supone" and, asked whether `<repo>/.longterm-mem/vault` is git-ignored or versioned, "me gustaria que viaje con el repo de forma opcional".
  - Found (read): a vault is the `--vault` flag, else `LONGTERM_MEM_VAULT`, else the project's registry row (`internal/vaultreg/registry.go:71-84`); with none, `ErrVaultNotConfigured`, exit 3 (`cmd/longterm-mem/exit_codes.go:113`), which the hook logs `skip:no-vault` (`engine/synctrigger/synctrigger.go:100`, `:333-334`). The registry is seeded from `Defaults` (one row, `labdrian-sdd-overlay` -> `~/labdrian-brain`, `Seeded: true`, `vault_registry.go:46-55`) only when no registry file exists and only by a command that writes (`internal/vaultreg/vaults.go:37-43`, `:76-88`); `A registry that exists is never touched, even one that lacks the default row: deleting a row means "not configured", not "seed it again".` (`vaults.go:38-40`), and the canonical requirements say so (`openspec/specs/longterm-mem-memory-access/spec.md:105-137`, R-022 and R-023, whose scenario "Unconfigured, non-default project is rejected, not guessed" this spec contradicts). `engine/synctrigger` resolves no project: it runs `longterm-mem sync` in the session's directory (`synctrigger.go:303`) and reads longterm-mem's `could not be resolved from the working directory` marker (`:132`); the project and its repository come from `internal/projectid` (`Resolve`, `projectid.go:127-133`), whose `Identity` carries `WorktreeRoot` (different in each linked worktree) and `CommonDir`, and whose chain (`identity/resolve/resolve.go`) already knows the main checkout's root (`Repository.MainWorktreeRoot`, `mainRoot`, `resolve.go:150`, `:179`). A vault is a claude-obsidian vault: the index rebuild runs the vault's own `bin/setup-retrieve.sh` (`internal/vault/index.go:22`), and a vault without it fails the rebuild (exit 5; C22-FU L14's differential, `--vault /nonexistent/x`).
  - Scope (the shaper's proposal for what L9 leaves to it):
    - (a) Where: `<main checkout root>/.longterm-mem/vault`, the main checkout's root and not the worktree's, so every linked worktree of a repository shares one vault, as the project identity does (`projectid.go:110-115`: the worktree root "is the value that fragments"). The name is the coordinator's example (L9).
    - (b) When: the default applies to a project with no registry row, no `--vault` and no `LONGTERM_MEM_VAULT`, whose working directory is inside a git repository whose resolved project is the project asked for (a `--project` naming another project, a directory outside any repository, or a bare repository get no default and stay "not configured", R-023). A registry row always wins (L9: existing rows such as `labdrian-sdd-overlay` -> `~/labdrian-brain` and `memorycard` -> `~/memorycard-lab/vault` are kept). Nobody is asked: the first command that writes for the project creates the vault (L8, "vos no tendrias que decidir crearlo o no"); a command that only reads answers the same path and creates nothing, the rule `ToRead`/`ToWrite` already follow.
    - (c) Git-ignored by default without touching the project's files: when longterm-mem creates `.longterm-mem/`, it writes `.longterm-mem/.gitignore` holding `*`, which ignores the whole directory, that file included. The repository's own `.gitignore` and `.git/info/exclude` are never read or written.
    - (d) Versioning is opt-in by the project (L9, "de forma opcional"): the project deletes or edits `.longterm-mem/.gitignore` and commits the vault. longterm-mem writes that file only when it creates `.longterm-mem/`, never into an existing one, so a project's choice is never undone, and a clone that receives a committed vault keeps it versioned.
    - (e) A created vault is usable at once: `sync` on it exits 0 and its index rebuild runs. Unverified premise: how a claude-obsidian vault with its `bin/setup-retrieve.sh` is provisioned (the two existing vaults were made by hand); T9's first step finds it, read-only, and records it before RED.
    - (f) The interaction with "a deleted row means not configured" was the open question of L12, answered by L17 (a): a deleted registry row falls back to the default vault.
  - Acceptance: RED per rule, through the command and over scratch repositories (`mktemp -d`, `HOME` and `LONGTERM_MEM_VAULTS_FILE` in scratch): a writing command in a repository with no row creates `<main root>/.longterm-mem/vault` and `.longterm-mem/.gitignore` with `*`, and `git status --short` of that repository prints nothing new; a linked worktree resolves the main checkout's vault; a read-only command creates nothing; a row wins; an existing `.longterm-mem/` without the ignore file is left without it (versioned vault); outside a repository and with a `--project` naming another project, exit 3 as today; the created vault syncs with exit 0. The registry tests of rows, seeding and `ToRead`/`ToWrite` pass unchanged except what the L17 (a) answer changes. R-022 and R-023 state the default vault, each with a "(Previously: ...)" note; README names the default vault and the opt-in.

- **L2** (2026-11-12, coordinator): origin. C22-FU T4 (`odd/tasks/phase9-c22fu-memorycard-compat.md`, L20-L21) built a one-time migration of the ten pre-memorycard pages (c-000112..c-000121). The owner's rehearsal on copies showed it correct, but a migrated page loses `engram_id`, the key the Engram path finds pages by, and the hook-driven sync (`engine/synctrigger/synctrigger.go:115`, plain `longterm-mem sync`, Engram source) would re-promote all ten (`ingested/` topics are eligible): "would promote 0 observation(s)" before, "would promote 10 observation(s)" after. Coupling found: the code is hexagonal at the import level (only `cmd/longterm-mem` imports `internal/memorycard`), but the domain keeps two parallel models (`memory.Observation`, `memory.CardMemory` in `internal/memory/card.go`), parallel walks (`sync.go`/`propagate.go` vs `sync_cards.go`/`propagate_cards.go`/`card.go`), store-specific page identity keys (`engram_id`, `memorycard_id` across 8 files of `internal/promote`) and a closed `--source engram|memorycard`. Facts the design must hold: Engram is frozen read-only since 2026-11-04 (no new observations); the vault's `wiki/memory/` and `.raw/.longterm-mem-manifest.json` are not git-tracked (C22-FU L17), so any page-format migration needs its own backup and a rehearsal on copies; memorycard supersede/archive add no version (C22-FU L19); R-007 eligibility (`internal/promote/eligible.go`) is shared; the vault holds ~600 pages carrying `engram_id`, 51 + 10 with `**Source-Id**`, and the live count must be measured, not assumed. C22-FU T4 is held, T5 and T6 wait on this feature's spec changes.
- **L3** (2026-11-12, shaper): Specs S1-S8 and tasks T1-T8 written; this entry holds their design. Read: this document, the goal file, C22-FU's document on this branch (to L19-fix) and on `review-43a753644421` (L20; L21 was not found in any file or in memorycard, so L2's summary of it is used), `internal/memory/{card,observation}.go`, `internal/promote/{memory,sync,sync_cards,card,store,frontmatter,propagate}.go` (parts), `cmd/longterm-mem/{cmd_sync,vault_registry}.go`, `engine/synctrigger/synctrigger.go`, the requirement list of `openspec/specs/longterm-mem-promotion/spec.md`, and `rg` for every reader of `engram_id`, `memorycard_id`, `engram_revision` and `engram_sync_id`. CodeGraph has no index in this worktree, so plain reads were used.
  - *Steelman.* Every class of defect C22-FU met came from the store being part of the domain: T3 had to extract `patchPage`/`savePatched` to share one patch between two walks, and its change-aware count and restore rule exist for memorycard only (C22-FU L19-fix); `reconcile` cannot adopt a memorycard page because it reads `engram_revision` (C22-FU L20); and the T4 migration opened a re-promotion hole because a page's identity is a store-specific key the other store's walk cannot see (L2). One model, one walk per pass and a store-agnostic page identity remove those classes instead of patching each.
  - *Chosen approach.* Model and adapters first, additive (T1); one page-identity reader that reads every key family, read only (T2); one promotion walk (T3) and one propagation walk (T4) over a port; the store registry at the composition root (T5); then the persisted format change alone (T6: writer plus the one-time migration, absorbing C22-FU T4's code); the legacy-key retirement only on the owner's word and live evidence (T7); the hook's source from configuration last (T8). The re-promotion hole is closed by lineage, not by keeping dead keys on pages: a memory whose lineage identity owns a page is bridged (skipped, counted, exit 0) in every source's walk, so a page re-keyed to memorycard is never promoted again by Engram, as S3 and S6 (d) require. Lineage is a property the adapters fill and `cmd/longterm-mem` composes (memorycard's `import_map` read both ways), so no adapter imports another and `internal/promote` imports none.
  - *Strongest counter-argument.* Engram is frozen and will never hold a new observation, so an abstraction with two adapters is built for one live store; the cheaper end state retires the Engram promotion and propagation passes outright, re-keys every page to memorycard through `import_map`, and deletes code instead of generalizing it. Answer: the owner chose adapters explicitly ("cada store sería solo un adaptador", L1) and prefers maximum decoupling; and re-keying ~600 pages to memorycard rests on `import_map` covering every promoted observation, which is unverified (C22-FU L15 counted 598 bridged memories, not the page set), and would rewrite page ownership the owner decided only for the ten (FU1).
  - *Tradeoff accepted.* The Engram adapter is kept for a store that only ever answers the same frozen data, and lineage adds one cross-store lookup per memory at the composition root (the per-memory `import_map` query is already a carried N+1 note, C22-FU L13).
  - *Found while shaping.* (1) Page identity has more consumers than `internal/promote`: `internal/ops/doctor.go` and `doctor_eligibility.go` join pages to observations by `engram_id`, and `eligible.go:55` (`PageObservationID`) serves them; S6 (a) enumerates them. (2) `engram_sync_id` is written on every Engram page (`frontmatter.go:200`) and never read back from a page (only blanked, `update.go:424`), so S6 (b) stops writing it. (3) The precedence sidecar is keyed by address and its `promoted_revision` field is already store-agnostic (`store.go:9-31`); only each migrated page's frontmatter hash changes. (4) The empty `sources` list key already sits in every page (`frontmatter.go:198`), one letter from L1's `source` (D1). (5) Today an Engram observation whose memorycard twin was promoted while the observation had no page would get a second page from an Engram `sync`; S3's lineage skip closes that too.

  - *Choices made for what L9 left open.* The main checkout's root, not the worktree's, so linked worktrees share one vault (the fragmentation `projectid` exists to prevent). The ignore is a self-ignoring `.longterm-mem/.gitignore` holding `*`, written only when longterm-mem creates the directory: the project's tracked `.gitignore` is never touched, a per-clone `.git/info/exclude` would not travel with a clone, and the opt-in to versioning is the project deleting that file and committing, which longterm-mem never undoes. No default outside a git repository, for a bare repository, or when `--project` names a project other than the working directory's: "dentro del directorio raiz" needs a repository root, and a vault created in the wrong repository is worse than exit 3.
  - *Strongest counter-argument to S9.* The hook runs `sync` at the end of every session in every directory, so S9 makes the program create `.longterm-mem/` in every repository the owner ever opens, third-party clones included, and a versioned vault turns every session's sync (pages, `wiki/log.md`, `.vault-meta/` sync state and index) into working-tree changes in the project. Answer: L8 asks for exactly this ("vos no tendrias que decidir crearlo o no"); the self-ignore keeps an unversioned vault out of `git status`, and the churn of a versioned vault is the owner's opt-in.
  - *Tradeoff accepted.* A directory appears in every repository a session ends in, and R-023's "rejected, not guessed" becomes "rejected only outside a repository".

(a) Recommended: the default vault is derived and never written into the registry; a row always wins, and a deleted row falls back to the default vault, so "not configured" remains only outside a repository (R-022 and R-023 change; the registry stays a list of exceptions). Consequence for S8: a project on its default vault has no row to carry `source`, so it syncs the built-in default source (`engram`, frozen, nothing new arrives) until a row names a source. (b) The first writing command adds a `Seeded` row for the project pointing at the default vault, so the row can carry `source` and a deleted row stays "not configured" as D5 says; but telling a deleted row from one never written needs a record of deletions the registry does not keep today.

Decision, not asked: `frontmatterRevision`/`frontmatterEngramID` read the revision and id of the page's Engram identity only, as they read `engram_*` only before, so a memorycard page is still refused by `reconcile` (`ErrUnusablePage`) and `entryFor` still records revision 0 for it (pinned by `TestPageIdentity_ReconcileStillRefusesAPageThatIsNotEngrams`); T3/T6 decide that. One behavior difference, by design: a page carrying `engram_revision` but no `engram_id` (hand-authored, no identity) no longer yields a revision; no test or fixture pins that case and every page longterm-mem wrote carries both.

Every Engram-specific piece of the promotion path exists only to keep a dead store coherent: the cross-store lineage composition, the degraded-open warning on it, `LineageError`, `StatusUnknown`, the `Prober`, the Engram propagation count D3 had to reconcile, the exit-4 label of D7. Most of T3's open findings are about that code (the Q3 table). One store removes those classes instead of answering each.
  - *Strongest counter-argument.* Retiring the Engram source does not remove Engram from longterm-mem: retrieval (query, get, embeddings, stale, status) still reads it, so the owner's "lo sacamos" is half-met by this feature and the adapter stays; and the "never promoted twice" guarantee for ~600 legacy pages now rests on two facts nobody has measured: that `import_map` names the observation of every promoted page, and how many hand mirrors and multi-part observations exist (L3 already marked the first unverified). Answer: retrieval is a different contract (`query` sources, the MCP `get` id, an embedding index keyed by Engram ids) and is R1; the two facts are measured by T6's dry run, which refuses by name instead of guessing, and T4/S10 (c) keep the bridge working in the meantime.

R2, `promote --id N` and MCP `promote{engram_id}` with no `source`: refuse with exit 2 and ask for `--source memorycard`, so no value silently changes meaning [recommended]; or default to memorycard, so N becomes a memorycard memory id (an old caller's Engram id would promote another memory); or translate N through `import_map` (keeps the old meaning, keeps an Engram concept). R3, the log kind of exit 4: rename to a store-agnostic kind with the golden [recommended, the label names a store the child no longer reads]; or keep `failure:engram-unavailable` (D7). R4, the optional registry `source` field of D4: not built while there is one store [recommended, L1's "si no se usa para que lo vamos a conservar"]; or build it now for a future store. R5, a legacy page the migration cannot map (no `import_map` row, or a memory that already has its own page): refuse by name and leave it for the owner [recommended, as S6 (c) states]; or map it by content hash. R6, landing T3: option (a) [recommended]; the owner may read L25's "ya no sigas metiendo cosas de engram" as also forbidding landing T3's reviewed Engram lineage path, which would make it (b).

In short: full-text `query` (Engram FTS5) -> a memorycard full-text read whose existence is unverified (R12); standings -> `superseded_by` only, conflicts and undecided lost (R9); embeddings -> the index rebuilt from memorycard's live memories, keyed by memorycard id; `get` -> `MemoryByID`; `stale` -> `ListMemories`; `status` -> memorycard reachability; adoption -> a memorycard query of the same question as `HasMemory`.
  - *Consumers outside longterm-mem, each its own task.* T18 memory plan (S14), T19 projection gate (S15), T20 skills candidates (S16). Found besides those three, comments only: `engine/agentgen/roles.go:15-16`, `:171`, `engine/reviewreceipt/reviewreceipt.go:73`, `engine/roles/filechain/store.go:61`, `engine/shaper/ports.go:46`, `engine/shaper/fsadapter/clearance_store.go:27`; they name Engram as history or as a place state is not kept and get no task (assumption).
  - *Order (internal/engram last).* T3 lands; T4; T5; then in any order T10, T11 (then T12), T13, T14, T15, T16, T8, T9; T18, T19 (after T13, for R8's `get` key) and T20 at any time; T6 after T10, T7 after the owner's live zero; T17 last, after every task that removes an importer (T5, T10-T16) and after T18-T20, so no Engram consumer is left anywhere when the adapter goes. T6, T7, T8 and T9 import no Engram and do not block T17.
  - *Changed consumed contracts and their Risk lines* are on each task line: T11 item 3; T12 items 1 and 3; T13 item 3; T14 item 3; T15 item 3; T16 item 3; T17 items 3 and 5; T18 items 1 and 3; T19 items 2, 3 and 5; T20 item 3. The independent verifier runs once on T17, T18 and T19, as on T5 and T6.
  - *Steelman of L27.* Retrieval reading a frozen store while promotion reads memorycard would leave agents searching a corpus that never grows and promoting from another; one store for read and write makes `query`, `get` and the pages describe the same memories, and lets the gate, the plan and the probes drop a dead source.
  - *Strongest counter-argument.* Engram's retrieval rests on capabilities memorycard has not been shown to have from this repository: a full-text index longterm-mem can read (R12), conflict and undecided judgments (R9), and a body format `stale` parses (S12 (e)); and the engine persists the token `engram` in workflow logs and handoffs (R7), so a removal that breaks reading an old log is a data defect, not a cleanup. Answer: each premise is a first, read-only step of its task (T11, T14, T20), recorded before RED, and each loss is an owner decision, not a silent change.
  - *Tradeoff accepted.* The open tasks grow from seven to sixteen and T17 waits for all of them; Engram stays importable until then.
  - *Unverified.* memorycard's full-text index and its schema beyond the three tables longterm-mem's tests build; that memorycard bodies carry `**Where**:`; that skills candidates and drafts exist in memorycard under the same topic keys; that the installer's owned-entry merge removes an entry it no longer writes (S15); whether the longterm-mem MCP registration passes `LONGTERM_MEM_ENGRAM_DB` (T17's item 5).
  - *Open decisions for the owner, new in L28 (recommendation first; R2-R6 of L26 stay open).* R7, a persisted plan source `engram` in existing workflow logs and handoffs: read it as `memorycard` [recommended, no data rewritten]; or rewrite the persisted files once (item 1); or refuse them. R8, the `engram_id` fields and `engram:%d` labels of `query` and `get`: keep the field names, carrying memorycard ids, and change the labels' prefix [recommended, decided together with R2 so one field has one meaning]; or new field names (`memory_id`) with the old ones refused; or accept both. R9, the `CONFLICTS WITH` and `UNDECIDED` annotations, which memorycard does not record: drop them with a "(Previously: ...)" note [recommended]; there is no other source. R10, the source names `engram-fts` and `engram-embed`: new names (`memorycard-fts`, `memorycard-embed`), the old ones refused with exit 2 [recommended, "si no se usa para que lo vamos a conservar"]; or keep them as aliases. R11, `status`'s `engram: reachable=` line and the `engram_reachable`/`engram_detail` JSON keys: rename to memorycard's [recommended]; or keep the keys. R12, only if memorycard has no readable full-text index: a substring scan of the project's live memories [recommended, read-only]; or ask memorycard's own search (its binary or MCP); or create an index (a write to memorycard's database). R13, the `engram:<id>` occurrence references of skills candidate records and the lint rule: new records reference memorycard ids and the lint flags both forms [recommended]; or leave the old references and flag only the new form.
  - *Commands.* None run by the shaper (reads and searches only); every test and check above is not run.

- **L46** (2026-11-13, builder, T4 correction batch, on top of `e66de6c0`; L45's header line, the tick and L42 and the header forecast were edited in place at the coordinator's request, the one exception to this log being append-only): fixes every finding of the review series and the two polishers, as new commits `0a958be8`, `21a621a8`, `4e4d81c1` and the docs commit that holds this entry; no commit rewritten.
  - *How memorycard splits an observation (read in `/home/labdrian/memorycard/internal/memory/import.go`, `recordParts`, `splitBody`, `cutRunes`, `importRow`).* A record is stored as one memory, unless its redacted, trimmed body exceeds the hard cap: then `splitBody` packs whole paragraphs (split on a blank line) into parts of at most 3/4 of the cap, joined by a blank line inside a part, drops the separator between parts, and cuts a paragraph longer than a part on a rune boundary (so a boundary is a blank line, or nothing, even inside a word). Parts are titled `<title> (part i/n)`, with the topic key `<key>/part-i` after the first, and import_map gets one row per part (`source`, `source_key`, `part`, `memory_id`). `importRow` reuses an existing memory of the project with the same content hash instead of inserting, so two observations can share a memory. Titles and bodies pass through `redact` (private tags become a marker), so an observation with private text cannot match a hand mirror's hash.
  - *Mirror recall, fixed (`0a958be8`, `21a621a8`).* `Store.ImportedObservations` (new file `observations.go`) rebuilds each observation's whole text from its imported parts in part order (the `(part i/n)` suffix of the first title dropped for several parts; the parts joined by a blank line and, as a second reading, by nothing), hashes it with `memory.CardContentHash`, and `Source` gives a hand mirror whose own `CardContentHash(title, body)` equals it, as mirrors, every memory of the matching observation in part order and then the observation. The stored `content_hash` of an imported memory is no longer read (a), an observation of several parts matches by its whole text (b), and the sibling parts of a twin are mirrors (c). Memories mapped by a source other than engram, and an observation shared by merged memories, are not mirrors; another project's imports and an unmapped memory are not either; an archived imported memory still is (a page outlives its memory). RED, observed before the code: `TestSourceMirrorsAreEveryMemoryOfTheObservationsWhoseRebuiltTextTheyHold` (memory 8, 12 and 18 mirrors empty: (b) with (c), (a) with an archived twin, and the part cut in a word); GREEN after. `MappedTwins` and `Twin` are deleted. The command-level helper `addHandMirroredMemories` no longer sets the hashes of memories 2 and 4, so the hand-mirror tests of `sync` and `promote` prove (a) through the commands.
  - *N+1, fixed.* A listing reads the parts of every observation it names in one chunked `MemoryIDsByEngramIDs` call, and the imported observations of a project once if a memory of it is a hand mirror (`Source.kinOf`); `ByID` does the same for its one memory. `bridgeOf` still asks once per memory, as at the base (`108bfa99`), and is not part of this finding. No test counts statements: the driver offers no hook without a production change for it, so the batching is pinned only by `TestSourceListingsCarryTheLineageAndMirrorsByIDGives` (a listing equals `ByID` memory by memory); this is an exception to RED for the batching alone.
  - *Command test, fixed.* The sibling-part test now calls `withNoEngramDatabase` and `pageOfMemory` matches the exact line `memorycard_id: <id>`.
  - *Non-numeric id, fixed (`4e4d81c1`).* When the pages cannot be listed, both walks keep the not-a-number error beside the listing's (`withIDError`); RED: `TestPropagation_AnUnlistablePagesDirectoryStillNamesAnIdThatIsNotANumber` (both walks), GREEN after. `TestPropagation_AnUnreadablePageWithholdsANumericMemoryAndFailsOneWhoseIdIsNoNumber` pins the branch of `bd07b1e9` (a numeric memory is still withheld, one with an id that is no number fails) and was green at once. `SyncFailure.ObservationID` is documented as 0 for an id that is no number, the error naming the real one; the type is unchanged. - **L47** (2026-11-13, builder, T4 second and last correction batch, on top of `68a21bb6`; L45 is not edited): answers the reviews of `0a958be8..68a21bb6` and the polisher of `0a958be8`. Commits `657a1710`, `51d9ef3f`, `dcc67685`, and the docs commit that holds this entry.

  - *Mixed boundaries and the single-part mirror, fixed (`657a1710`).* `memorycard.ContentKey(title, body)` is the one key: lowercased title and body with every Unicode white-space rune removed. It is computed on the hand mirror's own text, on every rebuilt observation (first part's title without the ` (part i/n)` suffix, bodies concatenated in part order) and on every imported part as stored, with its own stored title. A match on any of them makes the memory a mirror of that observation: every memory of it in part order, then `engram:N`. `/home/labdrian/.cache/sap-measure.py` applies the same rule. A white-space-free key can only add matches between texts that differ in white space alone, so a false bridge needs identical words in identical order. RED then GREEN in `internal/memorycard/source_kin_test.go`: a mixed blank-line and mid-word split (memory 22, observation 700), a hand mirror holding the text of one part (memory 23, observation 402), the suffix stripped when one part row survives (800) and when the single part's title carries it (801).
  - *Cost of the single-id path, fixed (`657a1710`, `dcc67685`).* `Source.kinOf` asks first, by the indexed stored `content_hash`, which observations hold the text (`ObservationsWithStoredHash`), and reads the project's imported bodies (`ObservationsByKey`) only when that finds nothing, once per `Source` (`dcc67685`; no statement-counting RED exists for the cache, the exception is the L46 one). Measured on a scratch DB of about 5,000 imported memories in one project (417 three-part observations, so about a quarter are parts) with the real indexes `idx_memories_hash` and `idx_import_map_memory`; base `108bfa99` / new: `promote --source memorycard --id` of a copy of one imported memory 11-18 ms / 15-19 ms; of the whole text of a 3-part observation 14 ms / about 0.40 s; of a memory matching nothing 18 ms / about 0.40 s. `sync --source memorycard --dry-run`: with hand mirrors about 0.36 s / 1.39 s (1.76 s before the cache); with none 0.29 s / 0.41 s. Every single-id promote is under 1 s, so no escalation. The dry run costs about 1 s more than base when hand mirrors exist: the index build is about 0.4 s and the rest is per-listing `kinOf` (about 0.27 s on a warm source, one listing of about 5,000 memories takes about 0.18 s by itself); this is left to the coordinator's judgement, not hidden.

The commit changes the shared `numericID` message, so its trailer `Risk: none` should read `Risk: 3`; the commit cannot be amended.

  - *`ContentKey` (A, B, G; `a0f764d5`).* A: the key is now the length of the title's key, `:`, the title's key, then the text's key, so a title and a text cannot trade characters. RED: memory 51 (title "ab", text "c") was a mirror of the imported "a" / "bc" (observation 900); GREEN after. B, kept, with the evidence: `ContentKey` still lowercases (`strings.ToLower`, per rune), because memorycard's own content hash does (`internal/memory/save.go:453-455`, `contentHash` lowercases after joining the fields) and the importer reuses an existing memory of the same project and hash instead of inserting (`import.go:237-246`, `importRow`), so an observation can be stored under the case of another memory's text and its hand mirror, in the observation's own case, would otherwise be lost, and the page promoted twice; S10 (c) states the same premise ("the same content hash"). `TestSourceMirrorsAreEveryMemoryOfTheObservationsWhoseRebuiltTextTheyHold` pins it (memory 53 against observation 901 stored as "MiXed CASE body"); it was green at once, since the behavior is retained (exception to RED: no behavior change). The Kelvin sign folds to `k` under `ToLower` as it does in memorycard's hash; that folding is memorycard's, not widened here. G: the doc comment of `ContentKey` says what matches (differences of white space and letter case alone), what does not (another word, another order, title and text trading characters), and the pre-existing limit: it compares stored text, which is redacted and trimmed on import (`import.go:165-173`), so a hand mirror that kept the unredacted text or the title the observation had cannot match. `/home/labdrian/.cache/sap-measure.py` still applies the L47 key (no length prefix, and it is outside this task's surfaces), so its fourth count can differ from the adapter's by the memories A now excludes; it is a measurement aid, not product code.
  - *Single part and repeated memory (C, F; `a0f764d5`).* C: the suffix is stripped from the first part's title only when the observation has more than one `import_map` row (`wholeTitle`; a part whose memory is gone still counts as a row, as the "Lone" case 800 pins). RED: memory 27 ("Solo") was a mirror of the single part titled "Solo (part 1/1)" (observation 801); it is none now, and memory 31, which holds that exact title, is its mirror. F: the whole text is rebuilt over every part row in part order and no row is dropped by memory id. RED: memory 46 (the text of observation 540, whose two part rows name memory 45) had no mirror; GREEN after. The importer cannot produce two rows for one memory when titles carry the part number, so this guards edited or merged data.
  - *The union (D, E; `a0f764d5`).* D: the stored-hash shortcut is gone; `ContentKey` alone decides, so the result is the union over every observation whose text has the key. Memory 43 holds the text of observation 530 (kept whole, its stored hash right) and of observation 531 (the same text cut in a word): it was found in 530 only (RED), now in both, in observation order. The stored hash is not a subset of the key's matches in one corner (a title and a text that only trade characters, which memorycard's hash joins and the key does not), so a union equal to what `ContentKey` alone returns needs the shortcut dropped, not kept beside it. E: `ObservationsWithStoredHash` is deleted, so there is no per-card query left to batch; `ObservationsByKey` is replaced too. `Store.ImportedParts` reads the project's `import_map` rows with their titles (the rows of memories that are gone included, as parts), `Store.MemoryBodies` reads the bodies of the candidate observations in chunks, and the hand mirrors of one listing are looked up together (`observationIndex.holders`): the index is keyed by the key of a title, since a `ContentKey` starts with the title, and a body is read only for an observation whose title agrees with a hand mirror's.
  - *Cache (I, J; `a0f764d5`, test `71cb65f8`).* I: the project's index is stored only when its read succeeded (`Source.indexOf`). RED, observed with the old pattern put back for the run (the cache assigned before the error is checked): `TestSourceReadsTheImportsAgainAfterAReadThatFailed` (an `import_map` row whose part is `x` fails the read and nothing else) saw no mirrors of memory 22 after the failed read; GREEN with the fix. J: `Source` holds a `sync.Mutex` over `kinOf`, its index map and its parts cache, and its doc comment says the cache is valid because `Store` opens the database read-only (`readOnlyDSN`) and a Source lives for one run. `TestSourceIsSafeToShareAcrossGoroutines` (cold Source, eight goroutines) fails under `go test -race` with the lock removed (`DATA RACE` in `kinOf`) and passes with it; its first version warmed the Source before the goroutines started and did not fail, which is why it now takes the expected answer from a second Source.

RED (three tests in `page_style_test.go`): the non-digit id failed `Is(ErrSyntax)` and `As`, the overflow said "not a number" and failed `Is(ErrRange)`; GREEN after. Risk 3 because the function is shared by both propagation walks, the sync and promote walks and the failure messages they record.

- **L49** (2026-11-13, builder, T4 fourth correction batch, on top of `94e96b7a`; L48 is not edited): answers the polisher of `a0f764d5` and the verifier's advisories A1 and A3. The behavior changes of `a0f764d5` against `61ec52e5` are A, C, D and F.
  - *Single-id row, accepted.* The coordinator accepted the single-id promote of a copy of one imported memory: about +10 ms, under 40 ms, because restoring the stored-hash shortcut would break the union of D. The T4 task line says so; the row is closed.
  - *How S10 (c) "the memories of its project with the same content hash" is implemented (A1; `74a36749`).* A hand mirror's holders are the union of two sets. (1) The observations one of whose imported memories has the same stored `content_hash` as the mirror's own stored `content_hash` (the hash memorycard computed, `save.go` `contentHash`: `strings.Fields(title+"\n"+body)`, so it ignores how the words divide between title and text). (2) The `ContentKey` matches of `0a958be8` to `a0f764d5`. The importer merges a record whose hash exists into the existing memory, which keeps its own title (`import.go:237-246`), so at `94e96b7a`, where only `textKey(stored title)` produced candidates, a hand mirror that kept the observation's title, or moved a word between title and text, was not found and its text was promoted a second time (the verifier's memory 11 had the stored hash of mapped memory 10). A holder of the first set needs no body, so the stored hash is read with the rest of the project's rows: `ImportedParts` selects `m.content_hash` and the index keeps `hash -> observations`, which makes zero extra statements per card or per listing (the coordinator asked for one batched query per listing; I measured that variant too, `Store.ObservationsByStoredHash` over `idx_memories_hash`, and it was no faster, 32-43 ms on the single-id rows, so it was not kept). An empty hash holds nobody; another project's import and a memory mapped by another source are not read (an `ImportedParts` row is of source engram and of the project); a title and text that trade characters inside a word ("ab" / "c" against "a" / "bc"), which memorycard hashes differently, still do not bridge. Why `ContentKey` stays (the verifier's advisory A2): the importer cuts a long record into parts at a blank line or in the middle of a word (`import.go` `splitBody`, `cutRunes`), so the stored hash of a part is the hash of the cut piece and no stored hash is the hash of the whole text; a hand mirror of the whole observation is found only by the key of the text rebuilt from its parts, and a mirror of a part cut mid-word only by the key of the part's own text. The stored hash and the key each find what the other cannot, so the holders are their union.
  - *RED then GREEN (A1; `source_hash_test.go`, through `Source`).* At `2d3ae79d`: `TestSourceMirrorsAreTheObservationsWhoseImportedPartHasTheSameStoredHash` failed for memory 61 ("memory 61 mirrors = [], want [{memorycard 60} {engram 950}]": the words moved from the title to the text, equal stored hash) and memory 63 (the mirror that kept the observation's own title, "Shared" / "title w x", against the memory the importer kept as "Shared title" / "w x"), and `TestSourceListingFindsTheMirrorsByStoredHashAsByIDDoes` found 0 identities for 61 and 63, want 4. GREEN at `74a36749`. In the same test, green at once because they pin retained behavior: 65 ("ab" / "c" against 64, "a" / "bc", both with their real hashes: no mirror), 68 (another project's import with the same hash: none), 70 (a memory mapped by another source: none). The existing "51" case (empty hashes) still holds.
  - *RED then GREEN (finding 2; `imported_parts_test.go`).* At `94e96b7a`: `TestImportedPartsLeavesOutAnotherProjectsGoneRows` failed ("project p was given {Observation:960 Part:2 Memory:0 Title:}, a row of an observation that is not its own", and the same for observation 961), and project q's read held observations 402, 800 and 961 that are not its own. The cause: `import_map` carries no project, and the read took every row of source engram whose memory is gone (`m.id is null`) whatever the project. The fix keeps a gone row only for an observation that has a memory in the project (`exists` over the same table), so no observation that a project can match loses a row (the test pins that observation 402 of p keeps its gone part 3 and q keeps the gone part 2 of 960). An observation with no memory left in the project was already dropped by `newObservationIndex`, so no mirror changes; only what the index received does.
  - *Finding 3 (`2d3ae79d`).* The shared-`Source` test reports a mismatch with its own got/want error. The failed-read test asserts the first failure is the `import_map` read ("read an import_map row of project \"p\""); its injected row had to change, because it named memory 60, which is gone, and finding 2 correctly no longer lets a gone row of another observation fail p's read: it names memory 1, which is in p. (`2d3ae79d` was first committed with that test red and amended before this entry; no earlier commit was touched.)
  - *A3: a real sync with no page (investigated; nothing changed).* Scratch world `fresh` (the verifier's fixture, 15 memories, observation 10 as parts 1 and 2, no page, `LONGTERM_MEM_ENGRAM_DB` pointing at a missing file): the dry run says "would promote 15 memory(ies) ... 0 bridged" and the real `sync --source memorycard` says "promoted 15 memory(ies); patched 0 page(s); skipped 0 bridged", with one page per memory (`memorycard_id: 1`, `2`, ... `15`): the two parts of one observation get two pages, and so do memory 11 beside its stored-hash twin 10 and memory 7 beside 6. Head and new are SAME in every step of that world, the dry run agrees with the real run (15 and 15), and `memory.db` is byte-identical after every step. Cause: `walk` reads the vault's pages once, before the first decision (`walk.go:42-44`, `:57`; `sync_cards.go:31`), so a page written earlier in the run is not seen by the memories after it; only a page that existed at the start bridges a memory. The walk's contract says "a dry run predicts the run" (`walk.go:51-53`), and it does, so no change to the dry run is called for. Whether this violates S10 (c) is not clear from its words: (c) "a memory of part 2 of an observation that owns a page is bridged" and (d) "promote none of the memories whose observation (by `import_map`) or same-content twin owns a page" speak of a page that exists when the decision is made (the acceptance fixture is "legacy-key Engram pages"). `pre1` (page of part 1 first, then `sync`) bridges part 2 at head and at the change, so the outcome for a vault with no page depends on the order of commands. Making the walk see its own pages (an index entry per written page, and a simulated one in the dry run) would bridge part 2 to part 1's page in a fresh vault, and part 2's text, which part 1's page does not hold, would never get a page: a loss of content to decide, not a defect to fix inside a correction batch. I did not do it. Needs your decision (coordinator): (a) keep: a fresh vault gets a page per memory and the parts of an observation are separate pages, as at base; (b) bridge in-run twins of the same content only (`Mirrored`), never the parts of one observation (`Lineage`); (c) bridge both, accepting that part 2 of a never-promoted observation gets no page. If (b) or (c), it is its own task with a RED test and a Risk 3 commit.

    - `67b95a4e` polisher F3 and review F1 (readability): **fixed, `3a7a9fc6`.** The `memorycard_id` branch of `mapPage` now looks the memory up (`CardBridges.MemoryByID`, which `*memorycard.Store` already has) and applies the same checks as the Engram branch: the memory exists (new refusal kind `RefusedNoMemory`, "memorycard holds no memory with the id it names", shown when not zero), is of the project, has no other page (the page itself excluded by file name) and is not claimed by another page of the run; each refusal has its own reason.
    - `67b95a4e` polisher F4 and `2d0a2a23` polisher F1 (a crash between the page write and the sidecar write): **fixed, `3a7a9fc6`.** The order is now precedence entry first, then page. A stop between the two leaves a legacy page whose entry already records exactly what the rerun would write (frontmatter hash of the rewritten block and body hash of the page's body); the rerun accepts that page, finishes it and does not call it hand-edited, while a page that matches neither the old nor the new fingerprint is still refused as edited. A failed save restores the entry in memory and leaves the page unwritten. The errors name the backup directory and say a rerun finishes the page. Tests: `AFailedPageWriteIsFinishedByARerun` (RED: no rerun hint, no backup in the message), `AFailedSidecarSaveLeavesThePageAsItWasAndARerunMigratesIt` (RED: the old order wrote the page first), `ARecordedEntryDoesNotAdoptAPageThatIsNotTheOneItRecorded`. The restore steps are in the guide.

    - `67b95a4e` review-2 F3 and polisher F5 (multi-line or indented identity values): **fixed, `3a7a9fc6` and `b52d71a2`.** `simpleIdentityLines` refuses by name (kind Other, "an identity key is indented or its value runs over several lines") a page whose identity key is indented or whose value continues on an indented line; the status patch leaves such a page alone. Test `AnIdentityTheLineRewriterCannotRewriteWholeIsRefused` (3 shapes), and the same three in `TestWithStoreAgnosticIdentity`.

It now carries a comment saying so.
    - `67b95a4e` review-2 F4 (a page whose only legacy key is a non-carrier one): **fixed, `3a7a9fc6`.** Candidates are all pages of the project with a legacy key line (`carriesLegacyKeys` reads the raw lines, so an empty value or an indented key counts), not only the identity carriers: such a page is counted in `Legacy` and refused as Other ("carries legacy keys but names no memory"). Tests `TwoBackupsAtOneInstantDoNotCollide` and `AFailedCopyLeavesNoPartialDirectory` (both RED before), plus `AFailedBackupWritesNothing` now makes `.vault-meta` a file, since pre-creating the directory no longer fails.

The search below has no ceiling anyway, since a revision never exceeds the memory's current one; the measurement says that in practice it tries a handful of candidates.

  - *Commits (Risk is the trailer of each).* `1ed8ff95` fix(promote): recovery without a ceiling, half-migrated refusal, no-project pages reported once, top-level identity (Risk: 1,6). `4887cfb4` fix(migrate-card-pages): sync the directories that hold the backup, read each file once (Risk: 1). `69810a69` docs (Risk: none). `017df91a` chore(scripts) (Risk: none).
  - *Every id answered.*
    - `69316290` polisher F1/F2/F3 and reliability F1 (recovery search): **fixed, `1ed8ff95`.** The 10000 cap is gone. For a page naming an Engram observation the candidates are the memory's current revision first, then every revision down to 0, each with the memory's current type and the type the page names (no fixed list of three, no empty type unless the memory's own is empty). They are built one at a time in `recordedRewrite`, called only when the page does not match its entry and its body is the entry's recorded body; a page that matches its entry never reaches it. A page naming a memorycard memory has one candidate, its own revision and type. When the body is the recorded one and no candidate reproduces the entry's hash, the page is refused as the new kind `RefusedHalfMigrated` ("frontmatter differs from the entry and is no recorded rewrite", shown when not zero), whose message names `.vault-meta/card-page-migration-<UTC instant>/` and says copying it over the vault restores the page and the sidecar. The kind also catches a frontmatter-only hand edit of a page that never stopped (its body matches the entry and its hash does not): the two cannot be told apart, and the way back is the same. Tests: `AStoppedPageOfAMemoryWithManyRevisionsIsFinished` (the memory went from revision 15000 to 15003, past the old cap), `AStoppedPageWithAnEditedFrontmatterLineIsRefusedAsHalfMigrated` (a `title:` edit), `AnEditConfinedToTheIdentityLinesOfAStoppedPageIsAdopted`. **Outcome of the identity-line-only edit: adopted** (an `engram_revision` edited after the stop is finished like any other stopped page), because the migration rewrites those lines anyway and the backup of the run that stopped holds them as they were; the guide and the code comment say so.
    - `69316290` reliability F2 (a page with no project counted in every project's run): **fixed, `1ed8ff95`.** Such a page is no longer in `Legacy`, `Refused` or the per-kind counts; `CardMigrationReport.NoProject` carries it, and the command prints "N page(s) have no project (no project, not counted per project): <pages>" on stdout and each page on stderr with the remedy ("add a `project:` line to the page by hand, then run again"). It is left alone, and while one is left the exit code is 6, so the zero the S7 gate waits for cannot be reached by hiding it. The rehearsal labels it "(no project, not counted per project)" and its vault-wide count is the per-project counts plus these pages (the guide says so). Tests: `ALegacyPageWithNoProjectIsReportedApartFromTheCounts` (promote), `APageWithNoProjectIsReportedApartFromTheCounts` (command).
    - `69316290` risk-lens F1 (`mapPage` reading identity through the parser): **checked, a real defect, fixed, `1ed8ff95`.** `parseFrontmatterFields` trims every line and takes indented ones, so a page with `memorycard_id: 7` and a block scalar line `  engram_id: 5` read as two identities and was refused "at once". The migration now reads its fields from the top-level lines only (`topLevelFields`). Test `AnEngramKeyInsideABlockScalarBesideARealMemorycardIdentity`. The shared reader of the page index (`readPages`) still uses the plain parser, so such an indented mention can still make a page a spurious carrier in the promotion walk; that predates this task and is not changed here.
    - `5764ab28` F1 (both lenses): **fixed, `4887cfb4`.** The `os.Chmod` is gone: `durable.WriteFile` applies the mode exactly to the file it creates, through its handle, and the backup directory is new, so the target never exists. `TheCopyKeepsTheModeOfTheSource` still passes (0640 and 0604).
    - `5764ab28` polisher F1 (ancestors): **fixed, `4887cfb4`.** `.vault-meta` (the directory that holds the copy, which it may have created) and the vault root are synced with the copy's own directories. Test `SyncsTheCopyAndTheDirectoriesThatHoldIt`.
    - `5764ab28` reliability F2 (Windows): **answered, `4887cfb4`.** `internal/durable`'s own `syncDir` drops every error, so there is no error class to copy. The backup reports a real failure (a test makes one fail the copy and remove the directory), and `syncDirectory` returns nil on Windows and for `errors.ErrUnsupported`, `EINVAL` and `ENOTSUP`. This is by reading only: `GOOS=windows go vet ./cmd/...` fails today in `internal/vecindex` (`syscall.Flock`), so the program does not build for Windows whatever this change does.
    - `5764ab28` reliability F3 (read-then-stat race): **fixed, `4887cfb4`.** `readWithMode` opens the file once and takes the mode and the bytes from the same handle.
